# Test File Priority Inventory

Snapshot: 2026-09-19, source revision `528c6d62b`. **1,034 test files; 14,862 registered test cases.**

Post-snapshot update (2026-09-20): the original `pendingMutationOutbox.test.ts` coverage is now split across
`pendingMutationOutbox.test.ts`, `pendingMutationOutbox.intent.test.ts`, `pendingMutationOutbox.occupancy.test.ts`,
and `pendingMutationOutbox.projection.test.ts`; its replacement snapshot/work cases moved to
`pendingMutationOutbox.workCosts.svelte-node.test.ts`. The historical counts and rankings below describe the
snapshot, not these new file boundaries. See [Browser State Sync and Recovery](tests/browser-state-sync-and-recovery.md)
for current coverage ownership.

Post-snapshot bootstrap update (2026-09-20): the original `src/ts/bootstrap.test.ts` suite
now has **249 cases across 11 files**, including two new memory-rejection and writer-lineage
regressions. See [Bootstrap coverage ownership](tests/browser-state-sync-and-recovery.md#bootstrap-coverage-ownership)
for current file names and counts. The row below retains the historical 247-case scope and ranking.

Post-snapshot command update (2026-09-21): the original
`server/fastify/__tests__/commands.test.ts` coverage now has **247 cases across 20 files**.
The core transaction/initialization cases remain in `commands.test.ts`; domain cases moved
to focused files, with three independent message-finalization rejection cases added and
message write-isolation assertions strengthened. See [Command coverage ownership](tests/persistence-commands-and-events.md#command-coverage-ownership).
The row below retains the historical 244-case scope and ranking.

This inventory ranks the current test files by the product consequences their assertions protect. Each file received a Luna High evaluation of its actual assertions. Ratings are qualitative review judgments, not measured defect rates or evidence that the suite passes.

Order: **Critical → High → Medium → Low**, then **Frequently → Often → Occasionally**, then **test count descending**. Repository-relative path breaks remaining ties. For a file covering several behaviors, severity follows the most serious credible direct consequence and frequency follows that severity-determining scenario.

## Rating rubric

| Severity | Protected consequence |
| --- | --- |
| Critical | Irreversible user-data loss/corruption, authorization boundary breach, secret disclosure, or whole-product inability to start/access persisted data. |
| High | Broken core chat, generation, editing, saving, or recovery; recoverable data inconsistency; serious prompt/provider execution errors. |
| Medium | Bounded optional feature, settings/editor/navigation/accessibility failure with a workaround, or localized performance degradation. |
| Low | Cosmetic or presentation edge cases, or development/documentation/architecture guards without a direct runtime failure. |

| Frequency | Exposure to the covered scenario if a regression exists |
| --- | --- |
| Frequently | Routine startup, reads/writes, messaging, or common navigation. |
| Often | Recurring feature use, realistic retry/reconnect, editing, or conflict scenarios. |
| Occasionally | Opt-in feature/provider, import/restore/migration, malformed/adversarial input, rare race, unusual configuration, stress, or development-only operation. |

Frequency estimates scenario exposure, **not the probability of a regression, historical incident frequency, or how often a test runs**. A security boundary can be Critical even when its triggering scenario is Occasionally.

## Scope and counting

Includes current Vitest `*.test.ts` files, Playwright `*.spec.ts` files, and the three test-registering compatibility `*.runner.ts` files. Test fixtures, setup files, helpers, snapshots, and orchestration scripts without registered tests are excluded. Baseline compatibility tests remain included because the current repository owns and invokes them against its separately prepared baseline.

Counts come from Vitest and Playwright collection without executing test bodies. Frontend collection enables performance gates and includes UI-map files; server collection uses its own config. Playwright counts each registered case once for the configured project. Parameterized and helper-generated registered cases count individually; multiple assertions or loop iterations inside one case do not. Registered skipped cases remain part of the inventory, so these are not pass totals.

Five specialized compatibility files are counted from their registration expressions and fixture matrices rather than the ordinary runner lists: `phase9CbsBaseline.test.ts`, `phase9LuaFailureSemantics.test.ts`, `baseline.runner.ts`, `cluster10.runner.ts`, and `current.runner.ts`, all under `test/compat-harness/`. Their specialized baseline/provider lanes were not executed for this documentation task. The source-expanded counts are 11, 2, 16, 2, and 16 respectively (47 cases total).

See the [Test Suite Guide](tests/README.md) for behavior-oriented discovery and [Testing And Operations](structure/testing-and-operations.md) for commands and lane ownership. This is a dated priority snapshot; recollect counts and reassess affected files when tests or protected behavior change.
